# Skills · R · part 104

740 entries, rfc-writer to rhoai-cve-analysis. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **rfc-writer**: Use sempre que o usuário pedir para escrever, criar, estruturar, preencher ou revisar um RFC, design doc, ou proposta técnica. Também ativa se o usuário mencionar 'RFC', 'design doc' ou pedir para documentar uma decisão técnica/arquitetural. Garante que o documento segue o template e o padrão de qualidade da empresa. ([alpheres/alpheres-toolkit](https://github.com/alpheres/alpheres-toolkit/tree/HEAD/plugins/rfc-writer/skills/rfc-writer))
- **rfc-writer**: Write well-structured RFCs with normative clauses. - **rfc-writer**: 프로젝트의 RFC(Request for Comments) 문서를 작성, 편집, 관리하는 스킬. 사용자가 'RFC 작성', 'RFC 써줘', '기술 결정 문서', '아키텍처 결정 기록', 'ADR', '의사결정 문서', '왜 이 기술을 선택했는지 문서화', '설계 제안서', '기술 제안', 'design proposal' 등을 요청할 때 반드시 이 스킬을 사용한다. 새 RFC를 작성하거나, 기존 RFC의 상태를 변경하거나, RFC 목록을 정리하거나, RFC 템플릿을 생성하는 모든 작업에 이 스킬을 트리거한다. 단순 코드 작성이나 버그 수정 요청에는 사용하지 않는다. ([nanana3679/hada-reboot](https://github.com/nanana3679/hada-reboot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/rfc-writer))
- **rfc-writer**: Draft a Request for Comments document — a structured proposal for an org-wide change that needs review and discussion before becoming a decision. RFCs go further than ADRs because they propose (not declare) and emphasize the rollout, rollback, risk, and migration mechanics that org-wide changes require. Distinct from ADRs (technical decisions) and decision memos (leadership-facing asks). Output lands as a `decisions/` note with status `proposed`. Use when proposing a change that affects more than one team and requires comment + revision before commitment. ([the-marmack/research-bot](https://github.com/the-marmack/research-bot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/rfc-writer))
- **rfc-writer**: Writes engineering RFCs as specification documents optimized for progressive disclosure. Leads with user experience, uses concrete examples, and splits large specs into numbered subpages. Use this to write engineering RFCs that will ultimately be implemented by a coding agent. ([wadejensen/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/wadejensen/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/rfc-writer))
- **rfc-writing**: Author RFCs — design docs that propose a non-trivial change before implementation, surface alternatives, and invite critique, in docs/rfcs/NNNN-*.md ([HigorAlves/orc](https://github.com/HigorAlves/orc/tree/HEAD/orc/skills/rfc-writing) · ★7 · 2 copies)
- **rfc-writing**: Write, rewrite, shorten or update the status of an RFC (request for comments) or design doc on Confluence, so it reads in 2–3 minutes with the detail in collapsed sections. - **rfc4226**: RFC 4226 - HOTP: An HMAC-Based One-Time Password Algorithm. Defines the standard algorithm for generating one-time password values using HMAC. Use when working with HOTP, one-time passwords, HMAC-based authentication, two-factor authentication systems, or OATH standards. ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc4226) · ★14)
- **rfc6238**: RFC 6238 - TOTP: Time-Based One-Time Password Algorithm. Extends HOTP (RFC 4226) by using time as the moving factor instead of a counter, enabling time-synchronized OTP generation. Use when working with TOTP, time-based authentication, Google Authenticator, or OATH time-based standards. ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc6238) · ★14)
- **rfc6749**: RFC 6749: The OAuth 2.0 Authorization Framework. Defines the protocol for third-party application access to HTTP services on behalf of a resource owner, introducing authorization layers, access tokens, and refresh tokens. Replaces OAuth 1.0 (RFC 5849). ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc6749) · ★14)
- **rfc7523**: RFC 7523: JSON Web Token (JWT) Profile for OAuth 2.0 Client Authentication and Authorization Grants. Profiles the OAuth Assertion Framework (RFC 7521) to define (1) the jwt-bearer authorization grant for requesting an access token from an existing trust relationship without a user-approval step, and (2) JWT-based client authentication at the token endpoint via client_assertion. The basis for the private_key_jwt client authentication method. Use when implementing or validating signed JWT assertions for OAuth token requests, client_assertion_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:client-assertion-type:jwt-… ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc7523) · ★14)
- **rfc7541-hpack-decode-limit**: Audit whether the HPACK decoder bounds integer/string representations — overlong integer encodings and oversized string literals must be treated as decoding errors, otherwise a single header block can exhaust memory or bypass length limits (CVE-2026-59248 cowlib HPACK/QPACK integer-decoder memory-exhaustion DoS; Jetty OSTIF audit TOB:JETTY-1 HpackDecoder integer overflow past maxHeaderSize; h2o issue ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc7541-hpack-decode-limit))
- **rfc7541-hpack-decompression-accounting**: Audit the resource-accounting model on the HPACK decode side — the 4KiB dynamic-table cap does not cover per-entry materialization during decoding; index-reference amplification (1-byte reference → full header copy) combined with per-entry bookkeeping overhead becomes an HTTP/2 Bomb (CVE-2026-49975 Apache httpd / CVE-2026-47774 Envoy, full stack nginx/IIS/Pingora, 32GB in 20 seconds per connection); classic HPACK Bomb lineage CVE-2016-6581 (python-hpack) / CVE-2016-1544 (nghttpd) / CVE-2025-53020 (Apache ~4000x) / CVE-2026-10725 (Perl Protocol::HTTP2 advertises header-list limit but never enf… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc7541-hpack-decompression-accounting))
- **rfc7541-hpack-index-address-space**: Audit HPACK index address-space (static table 61 entries + dynamic table) boundary and lifecycle consistency — out-of-bounds index reads crash remotely (CVE-2018-14645 HAProxy &lt;1.8.14 hpack_valid_idx() OOB-read DoS, CWE-125, RHSA-2018:2882); before a SETTINGS_HEADER_TABLE_SIZE=0 advertisement takes effect the encoder is entitled to use the 4096-default table, and a decoder that rejects early becomes an interoperability bomb (Zephyr ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc7541-hpack-index-address-space))
- **rfc7541-hpack-table-capacity**: Audit HPACK dynamic-table capacity negotiation and size-update timing per RFC 7541 §4.2/§6.3 — encoder-chosen values above the SETTINGS limit must be decoding errors, size updates must appear at the start of the first header block after a change, and multiple changes must signal the smallest value (at most two updates); divergent interpretations become COMPRESSION_ERROR interoperability bombs and table-eviction holes (related nghttp2/nghttp2#1660 interpretation-divergence empirical finding; Google Cloud ALB temporarily degraded dynamic tables to mitigate a client bug; apache.hc.core5 5.3.1 fi… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc7541-hpack-table-capacity))
- **rfc7636**: RFC 7636: Proof Key for Code Exchange by OAuth Public Clients. Defines PKCE extension for OAuth 2.0 Authorization Code flow, mitigating authorization code interception attacks by introducing code_verifier and code_challenge parameters. Enables secure OAuth usage for public clients like mobile apps and SPAs. ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc7636) · ★14)
- **rfc8176**: RFC 8176 - Authentication Method Reference Values. Establishes IANA registry for amr claim values in JWT tokens, defining standard identifiers for authentication methods including biometrics (face, fingerprint, iris, retina, voice), password, OTP, MFA, smart card, SMS, and more. Use when working with JWT amr claims, authentication method identifiers, or OpenID Connect authentication context. ([frankie567/reauth](https://github.com/frankie567/reauth/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/rfc8176) · ★14)
- **rfc9000-ack-range-validation**: Audit whether QUIC implementations validate ACK-frame acknowledgment ranges per RFC 9000 §13.1/§21.4 — acknowledging unsent packets, malformed ACK ranges, or undetected optimistic ACKs become a congestion-control-deception DDoS-amplification primitive (related CVE-2025-4820/CVE-2025-4821 quiche &lt; 0.24.4; ANRW 2025: 11 of 16 Interop implementations vulnerable) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-ack-range-validation))
- **rfc9000-close-draining-state**: Audit QUIC implementations' frame-handling obligations during the closing/draining states per RFC 9000 §10.2 — the closing state may only retransmit CONNECTION_CLOSE; the draining state MUST send nothing and process nothing; violations open the connection-close state machine as an attack surface (related CVE-2024-45396 Quicly draining-state ACK handling assertion failure, QUIC-FUZZ empirical; xquic Stateless Reset / VN log NULL dereference; nginx QUIC CVE-2024-31079/32760/34161/35200 single-session worker-crash family) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-close-draining-state))
- **rfc9000-connection-id-retirement**: Audit QUIC implementations' handling of Connection ID lifecycle obligations (NEW_CONNECTION_ID / RETIRE_CONNECTION_ID / active_connection_id_limit) for symmetry — asymmetric obligations create a memory-exhaustion attack surface (related CVE-2024-22189, 7.5 HIGH) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-connection-id-retirement))
- **rfc9000-flow-control-accounting**: Audit QUIC implementations' stream-level/connection-level flow-control accounting and limit enforcement per RFC 9000 §4 — accounting errors open memory-exhaustion or integer-wraparound attack surfaces (related CVE-2024-22189, CVE-2023-49295) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-flow-control-accounting))
- **rfc9000-handshake-done-timing**: Audit QUIC server handling of HANDSHAKE_DONE frame send-timing and receipt processing per RFC 9000 §19.20 and RFC 9001 §4.9.2 — timing and key-discard-order errors can be triggered by a malicious server/peer into assertion crashes (related CVE-2025-59530 quic-go, observed in the wild) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-handshake-done-timing))
- **rfc9000-migration-congestion-window**: Audit whether QUIC implementations violate the RFC 9000 §8.1 anti-amplification 3x limit and §9.4 pre-validation rate-limiting obligations during connection migration / NAT rebinding — sending at full speed to an unvalidated address opens a UDP reflection-amplification and traffic-hijacking attack surface (related CVE-2026-35191 OpenSSL QUIC non-validating-mode accounting error violating 3x; TMA 2025: 20% of IPv4 servers violate the amplification limit; HAProxy#3329 disable_active_migration over-interpretation empirical finding) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-migration-congestion-window))
- **rfc9000-new-token-replay**: Audit QUIC implementations' protection of NEW_TOKEN frames and cross-connection token replay per RFC 9000 §19.7/§8.1.4 — single-use constraints, the IP-binding MUST, and unlimited replay would let the address-validation system be bypassed (blind-spot clause; format custom + scattered MUSTs = high-variance surface) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-new-token-replay))
- **rfc9000-path-validation-amplification**: Audit QUIC implementations' enforcement of path validation and anti-amplification limits per RFC 9000 §8.1/§8.2.1/§9 — missing PATH_CHALLENGE/PATH_RESPONSE obligations or unenforced anti-amplification limits become an unauthenticated DoS amplification primitive (related CVE-2023-49295 quic-go, HIGH) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-path-validation-amplification))
- **rfc9000-stateless-reset-token**: Audit QUIC implementations' stateless-reset-token generation, secrecy, and collision handling per RFC 9000 §10.3/§21.11 — derivable / leaked / reused tokens let connections be killed remotely, and the Stateless Reset Oracle configuration surface is an amplifier (related CVE-2026-50009 Netty &lt; 4.2.15.Final; xquic/Quicly fuzz empirical findings) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-stateless-reset-token))
- **rfc9000-stream-reset-cancel**: Audit whether QUIC stream reset/cancel (RESET_STREAM/STOP_SENDING) state-machine validation and cost symmetry comply with RFC 9000 §3.4/§3.5 — Final Size invariants, missing STREAM_STATE_ERROR on state mismatch, and cancellation cost far below server processing cost (H3 Rapid Reset DoS surface; related CVE-2023-44487 same-pattern in HTTP/2, H3 stacks advised to mitigate proactively; 2025 MadeYouReset variant proves the family keeps evolving) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-stream-reset-cancel))
- **rfc9000-transport-parameter-validation**: Audit whether QUIC implementations validate transport-parameter integrity and consistency per RFC 9000 §18.2/§7.3 — missing/mismatched CID-related parameters, client carrying server-only parameters, illegal values not rejected (pre-authentication attack surface and MITM CID-injection surface; blind-spot clause; CVE-2025-54939 lsquic QUIC-LEAK evidences the same path) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9000-transport-parameter-validation))
- **rfc9001-0rtt-replay-protection**: Audit whether QUIC implementations enforce 0-RTT anti-replay protection per RFC 9001 §9.2 (MUST implement and use TLS 1.3 anti-replay) and §5.6 (application protocols MUST define a 0-RTT profile) — the same 0-RTT flight accepted twice by the same instance, or a non-idempotent request executed twice, is the replay attack surface (related quiche issue#2678 confirmed: 0-RTT exact-flight replay accepted twice, violating the RFC 8446 §8 at-most-once-per-instance MUST; anchor RFC 9114 §10.9 requires H3 to apply RFC 8470 anti-replay) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9001-0rtt-replay-protection))
- **rfc9001-retry-integrity-tag**: Audit whether QUIC implementations handle Retry-packet integrity and token lifecycle per RFC 9000 §8.1.4/§17.2.5 and RFC 9001 §5.8 — unverified integrity tag, replayable tokens, unguarded multi-Retry loops (pre-authentication attack surface; blind-spot clause; warpsock added §17.2.5.1/§17.2.5.2 loop guards as evidence) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9001-retry-integrity-tag))
- **rfc9113-content-length-data-mismatch**: Audit HTTP/2 implementations' consistency check between Content-Length and the sum of DATA frame lengths — in H2 Content-Length has no transfer meaning, but when forwarded/downgraded verbatim into H1 it regains semantics and becomes request smuggling (related CVE-2021-21295 Netty &lt;4.1.60 H2→H1 downgrade smuggling and the 4.1.61 follow-up fix; CVE-2026-58055 nghttpx ≤1.69.0 Upgrade+CL keep-alive smuggling; sibling/complementary to the H3-side rfc9114-content-length-data-mismatch) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-content-length-data-mismatch))
- **rfc9113-continuation-header-limit**: Audit HTTP/2 implementations' enforcement of CONTINUATION frames and header-block size limits — buffering unbounded oversized header blocks just to return a 413, or END_HEADERS never set, leads to memory-exhaustion DoS (related CVE-2024-27316 Apache httpd 2.4.17-2.4.58 nghttp2 buffering memory exhaustion; CERT VU#421644 frame-class disclosure; CVE-2024-24549 Tomcat and CVE-2024-30255 Envoy sibling variants) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-continuation-header-limit))
- **rfc9113-flow-control-window-stall**: Audit the symmetry and boundedness of HTTP/2 flow-control-window (WINDOW_UPDATE) implementations — window manipulation forcing the server to queue data at 1-byte granularity is Data Dribble (CVE-2019-9511, CVSS 7.5); zero-increment WINDOW_UPDATE is an illegal frame, and a server that answers each with RST_STREAM enables the MadeYouReset resource-consumption chain (CVE-2025-5115 Jetty GHSA-mmxm-8w33-wc4h); INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE=0 plus trickle WINDOW_UPDATE Slowloris holds form the hold half of the HTTP/2 Bomb (CVE-2026-49975/47774); RFC 9113 §5.2/§6.9 define window semantics but the "stalled-str… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-flow-control-window-stall))
- **rfc9113-goaway-drain-lifecycle**: Audit the HTTP/2 connection-termination lifecycle (GOAWAY draining and RST_STREAM races) — concurrent RST_STREAM and GOAWAY triggering a write-pipeline race can permanently block worker threads until the thread pool is exhausted (CVE-2026-12611 Jetty GHSA-gpg9-4257-cfm3: HTTP2Flusher.terminated reset to null by the race, queued items never processed); nginx graceful shutdown leaking GOAWAY on H2 connections causes worker hangs (2024-05 kaseiwang patch + http2-grace-shutdown-test PoC); RFC 9113 §6.8 defines the double-GOAWAY graceful-shutdown sequence, but the race window and termination one-w… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-goaway-drain-lifecycle))
- **rfc9113-h2c-upgrade-pseudoheader**: Audit h2c upgrade negotiation and pseudo-header validation — a reverse proxy forwarding Upgrade: h2c + HTTP2-Settings opens a raw-byte tunnel that bypasses authentication/WAF (CVE-2026-88008 Traefik GHSA-w4v4-9rw7-5326 CVSS 9.1 CWE-444+863, fixed in PR #13797 stripping the h2c token; CVE-2026-88011 v2 sibling; CVE-2026-73548 Envoy generic-upgrade smuggling); HTTP2-Settings must not be forwarded with requests (RFC 9113 §3.2), and pseudo-header rules (§8.3) are the audit anchors; BishopFox h2csmuggler demonstrated default forwarding in HAProxy/Traefik/Nuster as early as 2020 ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-h2c-upgrade-pseudoheader))
- **rfc9113-ping-empty-frame-flood**: Audit whether HTTP/2 implementations bound the processing cost of mandatory-response frames (PING) and empty frames (DATA/HEADERS/CONTINUATION/PUSH_PROMISE) — PING forces a per-frame ACK (CVE-2019-9512 Ping Flood: sustained PING inflates the response queue, exhausting CPU/memory); empty frames bypass flow control with asymmetric per-frame cost (CVE-2019-9518 Empty Frame Flood, disclosed with Envoy security-team acknowledgment); zero-length header memory residency (CVE-2019-9516 Header Leak); RFC 9113 §6.7/§6.1 give the semantics but no cost guardrails ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-ping-empty-frame-flood))
- **rfc9113-priority-tree-abuse**: Audit whether HTTP/2 priority-tree implementations resist resource exhaustion via cycles — continuously reshuffling priorities across many streams churns the tree structure and exhausts CPU (CVE-2019-9513 Resource Loop: nghttp2/nginx/netty/undertow full-stack impact, CVSS 7.5, fixed in nghttp2 1.39.2); RFC 9113 §5.3 permits implementations to deprioritize processing (blank-clause escape), but "if you keep it, it must resist churn" — the audit target is the boundedness of the priority state machine ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-priority-tree-abuse))
- **rfc9113-rapid-reset-concurrency**: Audit HTTP/2 implementations' concurrency-limit accounting and forced-close obligations per RFC 9113 §5.1.2/§6.4 — only open/half-closed streams count toward concurrency (closed releases the slot immediately), RST_STREAM closes without acknowledgment so "open then cancel" consumes no concurrency slot; without a connection-level work budget this is the original CVE-2023-44487 Rapid Reset vulnerability surface (Google/AWS/Cloudflare all affected, one of the largest L7 DDoS events in history, 2023-10; variants: batched delayed cancel, over-limit stream opens relying on per-stream rejection) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-rapid-reset-concurrency))
- **rfc9113-server-push-lifecycle**: Audit HTTP/2 server-push (PUSH_PROMISE / reserved-stream) lifecycle implementations — enabling push is an attack-surface amplifier: Cache-Digest header triggering a crash on push (CVE-2020-9490 Apache httpd 2.4.20-2.4.43, CVSS 7.5, mitigated by H2Push off); push-method access-control failure (CVE-2023-30631 Apache Traffic Server push_method_enabled config ineffective, CVSS 7.5); http4s Ember push-frame DoS (CVE-2026-69203, mitigated by proxy stripping PUSH_PROMISE); RFC 9113 §6.6/§8.4 require that clients can reject push and servers can advertise ENABLE_PUSH=0, but reserved-stream state-machi… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-server-push-lifecycle))
- **rfc9113-settings-frame-flood**: Audit whether HTTP/2 implementations bound SETTINGS-frame processing — repeatedly sending SETTINGS frames with an excessive entry count exhausts CPU (CVE-2020-11080 nghttp2 &lt;1.41.0: a 14,400-byte frame with 2,400 entries repeated saturates the CPU, impacting the entire ecosystem including Node.js/Oracle MySQL); the RFC only requires the length-multiple-of-6 check and in-order processing — an entry-count cap is the implementation's obligation (blind-spot clause) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-settings-frame-flood))
- **rfc9113-stream-state-machine**: Audit HTTP/2 stream-state-machine handling of illegal frame transitions — DATA on a half-closed(remote) stream and similar violations must be answered with STREAM_CLOSED, and manufacturing them in bulk becomes the MadeYouReset resource drain (CVE-2025-5115 Jetty GHSA-mmxm-8w33-wc4h); the HEADERS+RST_STREAM race with double callbacks and double cleanup can cause double-free (Apache h2_mplx.c, disclosed 2026); the RST_STREAM refused-stream path forcing an in-scope write can cause heap UAF (CVE-2026-56848 Node.js, fixed in commit ba6cb5c); sustained RST floods leaking memory on connection close… ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http2/rfc9113-stream-state-machine))
- **rfc9114-content-length-data-mismatch**: Audit whether HTTP/3 targets violate RFC 9114 §4.1.2 — the Content-Length value must equal the sum of received DATA frame lengths; a violation that gets forwarded implies an H3→H1 request-smuggling surface (related CVE-2026-33555 HAProxy) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-content-length-data-mismatch))
- **rfc9114-goaway-drain-enforcement**: Audit whether HTTP/3 implementations enforce GOAWAY graceful-shutdown semantics strictly per RFC 9114 §5.2/§7.2.6 — identifier monotonicity, rejecting new requests after receipt, and the conservative default on GOAWAY-less shutdown (evidence: quiche#2700, quic-go#5326, erlang_quic 1.0.0 fix; no standalone CVE) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-goaway-drain-enforcement))
- **rfc9114-grease-reserved-frames**: Audit whether HTTP/3 implementations meet the tolerance obligations for GREASE/reserved frame types, reserved SETTINGS identifiers, and unknown unidirectional stream types per RFC 9114 §9/§7.2.8 — unknown frames must be length-skipped, never rejected; H2 reserved frames 0x02/0x06/0x08/0x09 must kill the connection, never be GREASE-skipped; rejecting GREASE is a forward-compatibility break (evidence: aioquic &lt;1.6 stream-tail GREASE frame caused stream-data loss; confusing reserved frames with H2 smuggling frames opens a smuggling backdoor) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-grease-reserved-frames))
- **rfc9114-pseudo-header-field-validation**: Audit whether HTTP/3 targets handle malformed pseudo-headers/fields strictly per RFC 9114 §4.1.2/§4.2/§4.3 malformed judgments — lenient handling implies a parsing-split and H3→H1 smuggling attack surface (related nginx#1043 unfixed; H2 historical pattern replicated) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-pseudo-header-field-validation))
- **rfc9114-qpack-decoded-size-limit**: Audit whether HTTP/3 targets limit only the compressed QPACK frame length but not the decoded field-section size — no decoded-size limit means a compression-amplification memory-exhaustion attack surface (thousands-fold amplification per connection possible; related CVE-2025-64702/CVE-2026-40898 quic-go, CVE-2026-12523 quiche, CVE-2025-13945 Wireshark) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-qpack-decoded-size-limit))
- **rfc9114-server-push-frame-rejection**: Audit whether HTTP/3 clients/servers strictly reject server-push legacy frames — PUSH_PROMISE and MAX_PUSH_ID receipt must terminate the connection with H3_FRAME_UNEXPECTED (RFC 9114 §7.2.5/§7.2.7); silently swallowing them means a missing protocol-error obligation (evidence: quic-go#5686, inconsistent handling within the same function in v0.60.0) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-server-push-frame-rejection))
- **rfc9114-settings-frame-order**: Audit whether HTTP/3 implementations enforce SETTINGS-frame strictness per RFC 9114 §7.2.1 — SETTINGS must be the first frame on each control stream, and a duplicate SETTINGS must be treated as an H3_FRAME_UNEXPECTED connection error (blind-spot clause, no public CVE yet) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-settings-frame-order))
- **rfc9114-trailers-frame-sequence**: Audit whether HTTP/3 implementations handle trailer and multi-HEADERS frame sequences per RFC 9114 §4.1/§4.2 — DATA after trailing HEADERS is a connection error, trailer vs Content-Length consistency, missing validation of duplicate response header sections is an H3-layer message-smuggling surface (blind-spot clause; nginx#1043 same ecosystem; H2 trailer/CL historical smuggling pattern replicated) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9114-trailers-frame-sequence))
- **rfc9204-qpack-dynamic-table-capacity**: Audit whether QPACK dynamic-table capacity negotiation and size-update ordering strictly follow RFC 9204 §3.2 — over-capacity insertion and out-of-order size updates are encoder/decoder stream errors, a compression-layer state-machine attack surface of the same family as CVE-2025-64702 ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9204-qpack-dynamic-table-capacity))
- **rfc9218-priority-update-validation**: Audit whether HTTP/3 implementations' validation of the PRIORITY_UPDATE frame (RFC 9218 §7.2) is complete — frame position, target stream type, out-of-limit stream IDs, push-stream references, and priority field-value parse failures are all connection-error obligations whose absence is a blind-spot clause (erlang_quic Strict PRIORITY_UPDATE parsing fix as evidence) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9218-priority-update-validation))
- **rfc9297-capsule-content-length**: Audit whether HTTP Datagrams / Capsule Protocol implementations strictly follow RFC 9297 §3.2/§3.3/§3.5 — capsule coexisting with Content-Length and other headers, truncated capsules, and unknown capsule-type handling errors are an HTTP-smuggling same-family attack surface (same family: an undisclosed sharing-session smuggling case, CVE-2025-64702 pattern) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9297-capsule-content-length))
- **rfc9368-version-negotiation-validation**: Audit whether QUIC implementations handle version negotiation and downgrade protection per RFC 8999 §6 and RFC 9368 §4 — Version Negotiation packet invariants and the full set of Version Information validation MUSTs; their absence is a version-downgrade and parse-crash attack surface (evidence: QUIC-FUZZ xquic VN-event crash; CVE-2024-45396 Quicly same-family pattern) ([DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent](https://github.com/DangerousSONG/attack_skills_for_agent/tree/HEAD/rfc-audit-skills/http3/rfc9368-version-negotiation-validation))
